Schedule Reference Instructions
A
Line 1: Adopted/adjusted 
budgeted 
expenditures/expenses 
current year
Equals the total amounts for each fund type in the Adopted budgeted expenditures/expenses current year column
and the Expenditure/expense adjustments approved current year columns on Schedule E.
A
Line 2: Actual 
expenditures/expenses 
current year
Equals the total amounts for each fund type in the Actual expenditures/expenses current year column on Schedule
E.
A Line 4: Primary property tax 
levy budget year The entire estimated revenue from the primary property tax levy in the General Fund is pulled from Schedule B. 
A Line 5: Secondary property 
tax levy budget year
Copy the estimated amount of secondary property tax from an override election for the General Fund, as permitted
by A.R.S. §42-17201(A), from the applicable line of item 3.B on Schedule B, secondary property tax levy.
Enter the estimated secondary property tax in the appropriate fund type. The amounts by fund type will not populate
from Schedule B. Total property taxes to be levied in the budget year must agree with the corresponding amount on
line 3.C on Schedule B.
A
Line 6: Estimated revenues 
other than property taxes 
budget year
Equals the total amounts for each fund type in the Estimated revenues budget year column on Schedule C.
A
Lines 7 & 8: Other financing 
sources and (uses) budget 
year
Equals the estimated amounts of other financing sources and other financing uses for the budget year for each fund
type on Schedule D.
A
Line 3: Beginning fund 
balance/(deficit) or net 
position/(deficit) at July 1 of 
the budget year
When estimating budgetary fund balance or net position, which is different than financial statement fund balance or 
net position (see Budgeting FAQ #5), counties should review their accounting records and report all available 
financial resources, such as cash and receivables, estimated to be available at the beginning of the year or 
expected to be collected in the budget year. If the estimate results in a deficit in any fund, enter a NEGATIVE 
amount. Negative amounts will show in parenthesis and red font. 
Budgetary fund balance or net position should not include nonspendable amounts other than any fund deficits as 
described above. Nonspendable amounts include prepaids, inventories, and capital assets, net of accumulated 
depreciation and related debt, or amounts legally or contractually required to be maintained intact and never spent 
(e.g., principal of a permanent fund).
If the county maintains amounts in its fund balance or net position that it does not consider available to finance 
expenditures in the budget year, it must still include those amounts here and also identify those amounts and their 
intended purposes on Line 11.
Instructions Official County Budget Forms
3/22  Arizona Auditor General
#Navajo County - For Official Use Only

<!-- page 309 -->
Schedule Reference Instructions
A
Line 9 & 10: Interfund 
transfers in and (out) budget 
year
Fund transfers in and out are the estimated amounts that will be transferred in or out of the fund type during the
budget year. Interfund transfers are not expenditures, and the totals of transfers in and out for all funds must agree
with the corresponding totals on Schedule D.
A
Line 11: Reduction for fund 
balance reserved for future 
budget year expenditures
If a county has or considers that certain accumulated resources maintained in fund balance will not be used to
finance current budget year expenditures included on Schedule E, it should enter those amounts and their intended
future purpose here. Before reserving fund balance, ensure that the amounts will not be needed for current-year
expenditures. For illustrative purposes, rows and descriptions for fund balance maintained for future debt
retirement, future capital projects, and financial stability have been included on Schedule A and explained below.
Additional rows may be added for other specific reduction amount(s). For any added reductions, include a
description on the blank lines provided for the reduction amount(s). Reductions must be entered as POSITIVE 
numbers for the formulas to calculate correctly. 
Counties should budget expenditures for contingencies, such as unanticipated or emergency expenditures that may
arise in the budget year in each fund/department on Schedule E.
A Line 11: Maintained for future 
debt retirement Include amounts set aside for future debt service principal and interest payments on long-term debt.
A Line 11: Maintained for future 
capital projects
Include amounts set aside for the future purchase of land, buildings, building improvements, improvements other
than buildings, equipment, or other acquisitions that will be capitalized. Additionally, this category may include funds
set aside for long-term planned maintenance projects or future replacement of equipment.  
A Line 11: Maintained for future 
financial stability 
Include amounts set aside to manage cash flows in future budget years to cover such things as revenue shortfalls,
emergencies, and/or other unforeseen circumstances.
A
Line 12: Total financial 
resources available budget 
year
Equals the total of amounts available to be spent in the budget for the current fiscal year, in accordance with A.R.S.
§42-17151(A)(1).
A
Line 13: Budgeted 
expenditures/expenses 
budget year
Equals the amount of money required for each item of expenditure necessary for county purposes, in accordance
with A.R.S. §42-17102.
A Line 1: Expenditure limitation 
comparison
Equals the total budgeted expenditures/expenses for the current year and budget year from the summary schedule
above. The budget year also includes the total other financing used in the calculation.
Enter the estimated net reconciling items for the current year and budget year. Estimated net reconciling items for
the current year may be obtained from that year’s adopted budget. Estimated net reconciling items for the budget
year may be determined by preparing an estimated Annual Expenditure Limitation Report (AELR) as part of the
budgeting process. Enter estimated net reconciling items and estimated exclusions as positive or negative
numbers, as appropriate. The Uniform Expenditure Reporting System Forms and FAQs on our Office’s website
(see links to the right) include examples of reconciling items and instructions for preparing an AELR.
A Line 2: Expenditure limitation 
comparison
Instructions Official County Budget Forms
3/22  Arizona Auditor General
#Navajo County - For Official Use Only

<!-- page 310 -->
Schedule Reference Instructions
A Line 3: Expenditure limitation 
comparison Calculates budgeted expenditures/expenses adjusted for reconciling items.
A Line 4: Expenditure limitation 
comparison
Enter the estimated exclusions from budgeted expenditures/expenses for the current year and budget year.
Estimated exclusions may be obtained in the same manner as reconciling items described in Line 2.
A Line 5: Expenditure limitation 
comparison Calculates the amount subject to the expenditure limitation adjusted for estimated exclusions.
A Line 6: Expenditure limitation 
comparison
Enter the expenditure limitation the Economic Estimates Commission (EEC) provides. The total amount subject to
the expenditure limitation on line 5 must not exceed this amount.
B Line 1
Enter the maximum allowable primary property tax levies for the current year and budget year. The amount for the
current year may be obtained from that year’s adopted budget. Calculate the amount for the budget year in
accordance with A.R.S. §42-17051(A).
B Line 3.A
Enter the amount of primary property taxes levied in the current year and the estimated amount of primary property
taxes to be levied for the budget year. The estimated amount of primary property taxes to be levied for the budget
year must not exceed the maximum allowable primary property tax levy for the budget year recorded on line 1.
B Line 3.B
Enter the amount of secondary property taxes levied in the current year and the estimated amount of secondary
property taxes to be levied for the budget year. On the blank lines provided, list individual secondary property taxes
to be levied by fund, including those of special assessment districts for which secondary property taxes are
calculated. The amount to be levied as the result of an override election may not exceed the amount stated in the
resolution requesting voter approval of the county levy. Also, A.R.S. §35-458 requires that the levy for bond
principal and interest payments must be net of all cash remaining in the bond interest and redemption fund(s) in
excess of 10 percent of the annual principal and interest payments. Calculate and enter the total secondary
property taxes levied for the current year and the estimated total secondary property taxes to be levied for the
budget year.
B Line 3.C Calculates the total amount of property taxes levied for the current year and the estimated total amount of property
taxes to be levied for the budget year.
B Line 4.A
1) Enter the amount of primary property taxes actually collected from the tax roll of the current year. If the actual
amount of primary property taxes collected is not available, enter the actual amount collected as of the date the
proposed budget was prepared plus an estimate of primary property tax collections for the remainder of the fiscal
year.
2) Enter the amount of delinquent taxes collected from the tax rolls of fiscal years prior to the current year.
3) Calculates the total primary property taxes collected.
Property tax judgments Record the levy amount of any judgments expected to be paid in the budget year for an excessive property tax 
valuation judgment per A.R.S. §§42-16213 and 42-16214, throughout Schedule B as applicable. B
Instructions Official County Budget Forms
3/22  Arizona Auditor General
#Navajo County - For Official Use Only

<!-- page 311 -->
Schedule Reference Instructions
B Line 4.B
1) Enter the amount of secondary property taxes actually collected from the tax roll of the current year. If the actual
amount of secondary property taxes collected is not available, enter the actual amount collected as of the date the
proposed budget was prepared plus an estimate of secondary property tax collections for the remainder of the fiscal
year.
2) Enter the amount of delinquent taxes collected from the tax rolls of fiscal years prior to the current year.
3) Calculates the total secondary property taxes collected.
B Line 4.C Calculates the total property taxes collected.
B Line 5.A
1) Enter the primary property tax rate for the current year and the estimated primary property tax rate for the budget
year. Calculate the estimated budget year tax rate by dividing the proposed tax levy for the budget year on line 3.A.
by the assessed valuation, then multiplying by 100.
2) Enter the secondary property tax rates for the current year and the estimated secondary property tax rates for the
budget year applicable to all county taxpayers. Calculate the estimated budget year tax rate by dividing the
proposed tax levy for the budget year on line 3.B. by the assessed valuation, then multiplying by 100.
3) Calculates the total county tax rate for the current year and the estimated total county tax rate for the budget
year.
B Line 5.B
Enter the secondary property tax rates for special assessment districts for which property taxes were levied for the
current year and are to be levied for the budget year. Calculate the estimated budget year tax rates using the
allocation method that relates to the benefits received as determined by the county.
C Revenues other than property 
taxes
All estimated revenues other than property taxes must be identified on this schedule by source of revenue within
each fund. 
C Column: Source of revenues
Enter the title of each fund and its revenue sources other than property taxes. All funds must be included within the
appropriate fund type. 
Categorize intergovernmental revenues by source as well as by fund. List federal and State sources separately.
Voluntary contributions received under A.R.S. §§9-432 and 48-242 must be recorded on the applicable line in the
General Fund. Base the amount of the contributions on information transmitted to the Board of Supervisors by the
County Assessor or Arizona Department of Revenue, as applicable.
Revenues of special assessment districts not disclosed on Schedule B should be disclosed on Schedule C within
the appropriate fund type.
Instructions Official County Budget Forms
3/22  Arizona Auditor General
#Navajo County - For Official Use Only

<!-- page 312 -->
Schedule Reference Instructions
C Column: Estimated revenues 
current year Enter the amounts from the estimated revenues on Schedule C from the prior year’s adopted budget.
C Column: Actual revenues 
current year
Enter the amounts of revenues other than property taxes for the current year. These amounts include actual
revenues recognized on the modified accrual or accrual basis as of the date the proposed budget was prepared
plus estimated revenues for the remainder of the fiscal year.
C Column: Estimated revenues 
budget year
Enter the estimated revenues other than property taxes for the budget year. Do not include proceeds from other
financing sources such as the sale or refunding of bonds and interfund transfers on this schedule; include them on
Schedule D.
D
Other financing 
sources/(uses) and interfund 
transfers
Include receipt of monies such as those from the sale or refunding of bonds, loans, or installment sales of county
property; payments to a refunded bond escrow agent; and interfund transfers on this schedule, not on Schedule C.
Also include proceeds from sources such as bonds expected to be received in the enterprise funds on this
schedule.
D Column: Fund Enter all funds within the appropriate fund type.
D Column: Other financing 
sources budget year
Enter the amounts expected to be received in the budget year from other financing sources by fund for each fund
type.
D Column: Other financing 
(uses) budget year
Enter the amounts expected to be paid in the budget year as other financing uses by fund for each fund type. Other
financing uses must be entered as POSITIVE numbers in order for the formulas in the spreadsheet to calculate
correctly.
D Column: Interfund transfers 
budget year
Enter estimated transfers in and out for the budget year for each fund and the totals for each fund type. Transfers
out must be entered as POSITIVE numbers in order for the formulas in the spreadsheet to calculate correctly.
Transfers in must equal transfers out on the TOTAL ALL FUNDS line. 
NOTE: Interfund transfers are not expenditures and should not be entered on any other schedules. 
E Column: Fund/Department
Enter titles of funds and departments within each fund. All funds must be included within the appropriate fund type.
Several departments of the General Fund have been listed for illustrative purpose. 
An amount must be budgeted for unanticipated contingencies or emergencies in accordance with A.R.S. §42-
17102(A)(4). An example line item is provided for each fund type.
E
Column: Adopted budgeted 
expenditures/expenses 
current year
Enter the amounts from the Budgeted expenditures/expenses column on Schedule E from the prior year’s adopted
budget.
E
Column: Expenditure/expense 
adjustments approved current 
year
Enter the amounts of each departmental transfer the Board of Supervisors approved during the current year, in
accordance with A.R.S. §42-17106(B).
E
Column: Actual 
expenditures/expenses 
current year
Enter the amounts of the current year actual expenditures/expenses, if available. If the actual
expenditures/expenses are not available, enter the actual amounts as of the date the proposed budget was
prepared plus the estimated expenditures/expenses for the remainder of the fiscal year.
Instructions Official County Budget Forms
3/22  Arizona Auditor General
#Navajo County - For Official Use Only

<!-- page 313 -->
Schedule Reference Instructions
E
Column: Budgeted 
expenditures/expenses 
budget year
Enter the amounts of estimated expenditures/expenses for the budget year by department, fund, and total. Although
budgets for enterprise funds should be prepared on the accrual basis, to comply with A.R.S. §42-17102(A), include
estimated payments for capital acquisitions, and principal and interest payments on long-term debt in the enterprise
funds.
F Expenditures/expenses by 
department
Schedule F helps facilitate budgetary comparison reporting at the department level (the statutorily required legal
level of budgetary control) when a single department is budgeted in more than 1 fund. Total expenditures on
Schedule F should agree to total expenditures on Schedule E. Please note, if a county budgets each department in
only 1 fund, Schedule F may be omitted.
F Column: Department/Fund
Enter the titles of each department and each fund in which the department is budgeted. Funds in which the sheriff’s
department may be budgeted have been included for illustrative purposes. For counties that choose to budget for
contingency as a separate department, an example has also been added for illustrative purposes.
F
Column: Adopted budgeted 
expenditures/expenses 
current year
Enter the amounts from the Budgeted expenditures/expenses column on Schedule F from the prior year’s adopted
budget. If Schedule F was not used in the prior year, these amounts may be determined from the prior year’s
Schedule E for the departments that are budgeted in more than 1 fund.
F
Column: Expenditure/expense 
adjustments approved current 
year
Enter the amounts of each departmental transfer the Board of Supervisors approved during the current year, in
accordance with A.R.S. §42-17106(B).
F
Column: Actual 
expenditures/expenses 
current year
Enter the amounts of the current year actual expenditures/expenses, if available. If the actual
expenditures/expenses are not available, enter the actual amounts as of the date the proposed budget was
prepared plus the estimated expenditures/expenses for the remainder of the fiscal year.
F
Column: Budgeted 
expenditures/expenses 
budget year
Enter the amounts of estimated expenditures/expenses for the budget year for each department by fund. Although
budgets for enterprise funds should be prepared on the accrual basis, to comply with A.R.S. §42-17102(A), include
estimated payments for capital acquisitions, and principal and interest payments on long-term debt in the enterprise
funds.
G Full-time employees and 
personnel compensation
Schedule G helps facilitate reporting the estimated number of full-time equivalent employees and the total
estimated personnel compensation at the fund level as required by A.R.S. §42-17102(A)(1). 
G Column: Fund Enter the title of each fund. All funds must be included within the appropriate fund type.
G Column: Full-time equivalent 
(FTE) Enter the estimated number of full-time equivalent employees for the budget year by fund.
G Column: Employee salaries 
and hourly costs
Enter the amounts of estimated expenditures/expenses for the budget year for all employee salaries and hourly
costs by fund, including amounts budgeted for employee salary increases in the budget year. 
G Column: Retirement costs Enter the amounts of estimated expenditures/expenses for the budget year for employee retirement costs by fund.
G Column: Healthcare costs Enter the amounts of estimated expenditures/expenses for the budget year for employee healthcare costs by fund.
G Column: Other benefit costs Enter the amounts of estimated expenditures/expenses for the budget year for all other employee benefit costs not 
included in the previous columns by fund.
Instructions Official County Budget Forms

personnel compensation
Sums the amounts in the columns titled Employee salaries and hourly costs, retirement costs, healthcare costs, and
other benefit costs.

RESOLUTION   ___-22 
 
RESOLUTION FOR THE ADOPTION OF THE BUDGET 
 
FISCAL YEAR 2023 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with the provisions of Title 42, Chapter 17, Articles 1-5, Arizona 
Revised Statutes (A.R.S.), the Board of Supervisors did, on June 14, 2022, make an estimate of 
the different amounts required to meet the public expenditures/expenses for the ensuing year, 
also an estimate of revenues from sources other than direct taxation, and the amount to be raised 
by taxation upon real and personal property of Navajo County, and 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with said chapter of said title, and following due public notice, the Board 
met on June 28, 2022, at which meeting any taxpayer was privileged to appear and be heard in favor 
of or against any of the proposed expenditures/expenses or tax levies, and 
 
WHEREAS, it appears that publication has been duly made as required by law, of said estimates 
together with a notice that the Board would meet on June 28, 2022, at the office of the Board for the 
purpose of hearing taxpayers and making tax levies as set forth in said estimates, and 
 
WHEREAS, it appears that the sums to be raised by taxation, as specified therein, do not in the 
aggregate exceed that amount as computed in A.R.S. §42-17051(A).  
 
THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the said estimates of revenues and expenditures/expenses 
shown on the accompanying schedules, as now increased, reduced, or changed, are hereby adopted as 
the budget of Navajo County for the fiscal year 2022-23. 
 
PASSED, ADOPTED AND APPROVED  by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on this 28th 
day of June 2022.

Board of Supervisors Regular 7. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/28/2022  
Title: Public Safety Communications Sheriff's Office Mobile Application
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Presented By: Lt Alden Whipple
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and approval of a Sheriff's Office mobile application as a part of the already
approved Public Safety Communications ARPA project.
Background:
On January 25th, 2022, the Board of Supervisors approved the Public Safety Communications
ARPA project.  The Sheriff's Office has looked at a variety of products to help in the
improvement of resident access to information and services, as well as streamlining response
capabilities between law enforcement and the general public. The Sheriff's App is a sole-source
vendor offering push notifications, access to inmate housing, sex offender notifications and
information, press releases, and the future APPRISS database that will allow immediate victim
notification regarding offender releases and criminal justice information.
Recommendation: 
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office recommends that "The Sheriff App" be approved as a
qualified purchase under the existing Public Safety Communications ARPA project.

Pursuant to A.R.S. § 16-531
  
When an election is ordered, and not less than twenty days prior to a general or primary
election, the board of supervisors shall appoint for each election precinct one inspector, one
marshal, two judges and as many clerks of election as deemed necessary.
A.
 
Pursuant to A.R.S. § 16-411
  
Not less than twenty days before a general or primary election, and at least ten days
before a special election, the board shall designate one polling place within each
precinct where the election shall be held, except that:

RESOLUTION NO ______ - 22 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS, 
DESIGNATING POLLING PLACES/VOTE CENTERS, PURSUANT TO ARS § 16-411(B). 
 
 WHEREAS, ARS § 16-411(B) provides as follows with respect to designating polling places: 
 
         “Not less than twenty days before a general or primary election, and at least ten days before a special election, the 
board shall designate one polling place within each precinct where the election shall be held, except that: 1.On a specific 
finding of the board, included in the order or resolution designating polling places pursuant to this subsection, that no 
suitable polling place is available within a precinct, a polling place for such precinct may be designated within an adjacent 
precinct. 
         and 
 
WHEREAS, on a specific resolution of the board, the board may authorize the use of voting centers in place of or in addition 
to specifically designated polling place. A voting center shall allow any voter in the county to receive the appropriate ballot 
for that voter on Election Day and lawfully cast the ballot. The voting center may be established in coordination and 
consultation with the county recorder, at other county offices or at other location in the county deemed appropriate. 
       
  
 NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED by the Board of Supervisors as follows:  
 
 That polling locations/vote centers for all Navajo County voting precincts attached hereto and incorporated herein as 
“Exhibit A” and contingency plan herein as “Exhibit B”, are hereby approved by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors.  
 
           PASSED, APPROVED AND ADOPTED at Holbrook, Arizona on July 28, 2022 
 
  
 NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
                
 By ________________________________________ 
 Dawnafe Whitesinger 
 Chairwoman of the Board

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. a. 1.        
Meeting Date: 06/28/2022  
Title: ZO 22-003 AMENDMENTS TO ARTICLES 3, 4, 25, AND 28 NAVAJO COUNTY
ZONING ORDINANCE - USE PERMITS AND GUEST HOUSES
Submitted For: Berrin Nejad, Community Development Director 
Submitted By: Cody Cooper, Planner II
Department: Public Works Division: Planning & Zoning
Presented By: Cody Cooper
Motion before the Board:
Consideration and adoption of Resolution Number 23-2022, approving/denying text
amendments to Articles 3, 4, 25, and 28 of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, regarding Use
Permits and Guest Houses, addition of section to Article 25.
Background:
Use Permits for guest houses in Navajo County currently go to hearing in front of the Board of
Adjustment to consider approval/denial.  A.R.S. § 11-816 outlines the scope and authority of a
Board of Adjustment.  This section of State law does not authorize Boards of Adjustment the
authority to approve/deny guest houses for a jurisdiction.  As a result, the proposed Zoning
Ordinance Text Amendment looks for a way to address guest houses and remove this from the
scope of consideration of the Board of Adjustment.
Recommendation: 
Planning and Zoning Staff and the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission recommend
the text amendment to Articles 3, 4, 25, and 28 of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.

<!-- page 349 -->
NAVAJO N 
 
NAVAJO COUNTY 
 
               PLANNING AND ZONING DEPARTMENT 
       
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
 
STAFF REPORT  
 
TO:  Board of Supervisors 
 
FROM:  Cody Cooper, Planner II 
  
HEARING DATE:  June 28, 2022   
 
PROJECT NUMBER:  ZO 22 -003 AMENDMENTS TO ARTICLES 3, 4, 25, AND 28 
NAVAJO COUNTY ZONING ORDINANCE – USE PERMITS AND 
GUEST HOUSES: Consideration of text amendment to Articles 3, 4, 
25, and 28 of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance, regarding Use 
Permits and Guest Houses, addition of section to Article 25. 
 
 
 
BACKGROUND 
 
Use Permits for guest houses in Navajo County currently go to hearing in front of the Board of 
Adjustment to consider approval/denial.  A.R.S. § 11-816 outlines the scope and authority of a Board 
of Adjustment.  This section of State law does not authorize Boards of Adjustment the authority to 
approve/deny guest houses for a jurisdiction.  As a result, the proposed Zoning Ordinance Text 
Amendment would look for a way to address  guest houses and remove this from the scope of 
consideration of the Board of Adjustment. 
 
STAFF ANALYSIS 
 
Due to the way that A.R.S. § 11 -816 is worded, it is not within the authority of the Navajo County 
Board of Adjustment to continue approving/denying guest houses with their hearing processes.  Due 
to this, Staff has researched jurisdictions around the State of Arizona to compare processes and find 
a suitable way for guest houses to be addressed.  In these considerations, a number of considerations 
have been made and are addressed further within this analysis. 
 
The first concern that Staff considered for guest houses was how they could be addressed and 
reviewed to continue in the preservation of public health, welfare, and safety.  In considering this, 
Staff wanted to compare how similar jurisdictions consider an d permit guest houses.  Through 
research, Staff has found that eight (8) of the fifteen Arizona Counties permit guest houses by-right.  
With this, the jurisdictions will generally have some form of Administrative/Staff review of guest

<!-- page 350 -->
Board of Supervisors                                      June 28, 2022        2 | P a g e  
houses to ensure that their placement is appropriate and meets County standards.  Three (3) Arizona 
Counties have a form of Administrative Hearing to consider guest houses.  These jurisdictions 
involve the usage of an Administrative Hearing Office, as opposed to their Board of  Adjustment to 
consider the permitting of guest houses.  Two (2) jurisdictions currently require a Special Use Permit 
in order to consider guest houses.  T his means that these jurisdictions take guest homes in front of 
their Planning and Zoning Commission and potentially Board of Supervisors to be considered.  
Navajo County is currently the only County that brings guest house consideration in front of their 
Board of Adjustment. 
 
Currently, the largest complaint received for guest houses to Planning and Zoning Staff is the wait 
time to get in front of the Board of Adjustment to consider whether a guest house should be 
approved/denied.  Due to notification and advertising deadlines, it can take anywhere from six (6) 
to eight (8) weeks to get a guest house in front of the Board of Adjustment for consideration.  If the 
County wanted to move forward with Administrative Hearings or Special Use Permits being 
required, it would not be able to alleviate the above concern, as both would require similar 
advertising stan dards to those that currently exist.  If permitted by -right, and reviewed 
administratively by Staff, this process could realistically be cut down to two (2) weeks from receipt 
of application to full review.  This review time would be similar/slightly faster than the review times 
for the Navajo County Building Department for new construction, and thus would not cause any 
further delay for the permitting of guest houses within the jurisdiction. 
 
The current considerations made by the Board of Adjustment as to  whether or not a guest house 
should be permitted are as follows: 
 
- The guest house meets all separation and setback requirements of a residence. 
- There is proof of an approved waste system (septic or sewer). 
- The guest house is appropriately sized.  
- The guest house is consistent with the intent of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance. 
- The guest house will not cause an inappropriate impact to public health, welfare, or safety. 
 
Staff also makes the same considerations of applications for guest houses when preparing their Staff 
Reports, which are reviewed by the Board.  Provided all of the above criteria are addressed and met, 
guest houses have historically been approved by the Board of Adjustment.  As Staff already  
considers and addresses the above in their Staff Reports and recommendations for guest houses, it 
is well within the ability of Staff to complete the same review administratively.   
 
Neighboring input is currently considered for Guest Houses.  While all input is welcome, if it does 
not point out some form of violation of the above requirements, it does not have an input on the 
ruling of the Board of Adjustment for approval/denial of a Use Permit. 
 
In the past, Staff and the Board of Adjustment have ma de considerations and conditions to 
discourage and prevent the separate rental of guest houses on properties.  Within the past few years, 
Arizona has witnessed the addition of A.R.S. 11 § 269.17, regarding limits on regulation of short -
term rentals.  Following discussions with the legal counsel, it has been determined that is not in the 
interest of the County to continue with conditions regarding rental  of guest houses , as they could 
potentially conflict with this State Law.   It is due to this that any upda tes to how guest houses are 
considered would not longer include provisions that limit rentability of these structures. 
 
Based upon all of the above, Staff is currently recommending that guest houses be taken from the

<!-- page 351 -->
Board of Supervisors                                      June 28, 2022        3 | P a g e  
scope of consideration of the Navajo Co unty Board of Adjustment and become permitted by -right, 
provided a number of conditions are met.  This suggestion would allow for the following: 
 
- Bring the Navajo County Board of Adjustment in compliance with A.R.S. § 11-816 
- Expedite guest house approval/denial time from six to eight weeks down to approximately two 
weeks. 
- Continue the same considerations for guest houses to maintain the intent of the Navajo County 
Zoning Ordinance and maintain public health, welfare, and safety. 
 
The following additions are proposed: 
 
Staff is currently recommending that Articles 3, 4, and 28 of the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance 
be amended to reflect changes for guest house regulations and consideration.  Articles 3 and 4 would 
be amended to provide simpler lan guage regarding guest houses, with a reference to a newly 
proposed section within the Zoning Ordinance for guest houses.  Article 28 would be amended to 
strike any reference to Use Permits with the Board of Adjustment, which are the current apparatus 
for considering guest houses. 
 
In addition to the above amendments, Staff would be recommending the addition of Section 2520, 
under General Provisions, to be title “Guest Houses.”  This new section of the Zoning Ordinance 
would address performance standards, applications, permits, administration, and appeals of decision. 
 
Within the attachments of this Staff report, the Board will find a track changes and clean copy 
version of the amendments, as currently proposed by Staff. 
 
At the Planni ng and Zonin g Commission Hearing hel d on Thursday, May 19 th, 2022, the Navajo 
County Planning and Zoning Commission unanimously recommended that the Board of Supervisors 
approve the Zoning Ordinance text amendment, as recommended by Staff.

Board of Supervisors                                      June 28, 2022        5 | P a g e  
 
Attachment 1 – Track Changes 
 
 
Articles 3, 4, 25, and 28 are hereby amended as follows (new text is shown in underline, and text proposed 
to be deleted is shown as strikeout): 
 
Section 2520 – Guest Houses 
 
Performance Standards 
 
Approval of Guest Houses shall require compliance will the following performance standards: 
1. A maximum of one Guest House is permitted per lot. 
2. Guest Houses shall only be permitted in those zoning districts in which they are listed under Use 
Regulations as an allowed use.  The following are the only permissible zoning districts for Guest 
Houses addressed under this section: 
a. A-General 
b. RU-20 
c. RU-10 
d. RU-5 
e. RU-1 
3. Guest Houses shall only be permitted on lots which have a minimum lot size of ten thousand (10,000) 
square feet or more. 
4. Guest Houses are limit ed to seventy (70) percent of the livable square footage of the primary 
dwelling.  
5. Guest Houses shall meet all setbacks required of a primary dwelling in the zoning district in which 
they are located. 
6. Single family Dwellings, including conventional site-built homes, Manufactured Homes (including 
rehabilitated Mobile Homes) and Factory Built Buildings may be used as a Guest House in zones 
where those structures are allowed as primary Dwelling Units.  Travel Trailers and Recreational 
Vehicles are prohibited as Guest Houses. 
7. All utilities may be on separate meters than the primary dwelling, unless otherwise prohibited by a 
utility company. 
8. Whenever possible, Guest Houses shall not be located in front of the primary dwelling. 
Applications 
 
An application for a Guest House shall be submitted to the Director of Development Services on a form 
approved by the Director.  The application shall be accompanied by: 
1. An accurate site plan and description of the property, detailed description of the propos ed use, 
preliminary floor plans and elevations of all proposed buildings, and an estimate of the valuation of 
the proposed construction. 
2. Satisfactory evidence of the applicant’s ability and intention to proceed with construction in 
accordance with the plans within six months after the Guest House permit is granted. 
Permits and Administration 
1. Applicable Building, Environmental Quality, and Engineering Permits apply.  Community 
Development review shall be conducted through the issuance of such permits.

<!-- page 354 -->
Board of Supervisors                                      June 28, 2022        6 | P a g e  
2. A decision of the Community Development Director may be appealed to the Board of Adjustment. 
Article 3 
 
Section 302 – Use Regulations 
 
18. Accessory buildings and uses customarily incidental to above uses, including: 
 
b. Guest houses and quarters for servants and caretakers employed on the premises, providing that the lot 
be a minimum of ten thousand square feet (10,000 sq. ft.) in area, that all buildings meet all building 
setbacks, that the guest house or servants and caretakers quarters be no greater than seventy-percent (70%) 
of the square footage of the principal dwelling and subject to securing a use permit.  If kitchen facilities are

Article 28 – BOARDS OF ADJUSTMENT 
 
Section 2802 – Powers and Duties. 
 
Each Board of Adjustment shall have those powers and duties as prescribed by A.R. S. 11-807 816 
and the Zoning Ordinance, which are as follows: 
 
3. Use Permits. The board may grant use permits as permitted by the regulations applicable to the 
zoning district in which the property is located.  No use permit shall be granted unless the board 
finds that the use and the manner of conducting it (including any associated buildings or structures) 
will be consistent with the intent and purposes of the Zoning Ordinance and will not be detrimental 
to persons residing or working in the vicinity, to adjacent property or the neighborhood in general, 
or to the public welfare.  In granting a use permit, appropriate conditions to preserve the intent and 
purposes of the Zoning Ordinance may be prescribed (including, without limitation, the acquisition 
of right-of-way for street widening purposes in accordance with the recommendations of the Public 
Works Department if it appears that the use would increase traffic congestion).  Unless otherwise 
stated on the permit, a use permit shall be permanent and shall run with the land. 
Section 2804 – Applications

<!-- page 355 -->
Board of Supervisors                                      June 28, 2022        7 | P a g e  
1. Variances and Use Permits .  An application for a variance or use permit shall be submitted to the 
Director of Development Services on a form approved by the Director.  The application shall be 
accompanied by: 
b. Satisfactory evidence of the applicant’s ability and intention to proceed with construction in 
accordance with the plans within six months after the variance or use permit is granted. 
c. Reasons for requesting the variance or use permit. 
 
Section 2805 – Fees  
 
1. Variances and Use Permits.  The fees for Variances and Use Permits shall be in accordance with 
a schedule of fees adopted by the Board of Supervisors. 
Section 2806 – Public Hearings and Notice. 
 
1. Variances and Use Permits.  Upon receipt in proper form of an application for a variance or use 
permit, the Board of Adjustment shall hold a public hearing after giving notice thereof at least 
fifteen days in advance by posting the subject property and mailing notice by first -class mail to 
the applicant, and to the owners of record of all parcels physically adjoining the subject property 
and all parcels separated from the subject property by a public road. 
Section 2807 – Expiration of Variances and Use Permits 
 
A variance or use permit shall automatically expire if substantial construction, in accordance with 
the plans for which the variance or permit was granted, has not been completed within one year from 
the date on which the variance or permit was granted.  If an appeal is filed in accordance with Section 
2808, this time period shall be tolled while the appeal is pending.

not affect the validity of the remaining portions thereof.

or consistency, within thirty (30) days following adoption by the Board of Supervisors.

This ordinance shall become effective thirty (30) days following adoptions by the Board of 
Supervisors.

Board of Supervisors Regular 9. b. 1. a.        
Meeting Date: 06/28/2022  
Title: IGA with the Silver Creek Flood Control District Regarding Millet Swale - District
III
Submitted For: John Osgood, Public Works Director 
Submitted By: John Osgood, Public Works Director
Department: Public Works
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement between the Navajo County Flood Control District and the Silver
Creek Flood Control District for reimbursement of survey work completed on approximately 402
acres upstream from the Millet Swale Dam
Background:
In late July 2021, the Millet Swale Reservoir sustained damage from a significant rain event. 
Residents of unincorporated Navajo County, the Town of Taylor and the Town of Snowflake
residing in the area served by the Silver Creek Flood Control District (SCFCD) were impacted.
Recovery from the damage included: 
Stabilizing the dam breach1.
Installing flood detecting/monitoring equipment2.
Developing a topographic map of the upstream watershed3.
Modelling and developing downstream inundation maps. 4.
At its meeting of October 21, 2021, the BOS approved an IGA with the Arizona Department of
Water Resources that granted reimbursement potential for the cost of those activities incurred by
the Navajo County Flood Control District (the DISTRICT).  
Following the execution of the IGA between the DISTRICT and ADWR, the County learned the
development of the topographic map of the upstream watershed (item 3 above) was completed by
engineering professionals procured by the SCFCD.  Public Works engineering staff has verified
the accuracy of the topographic survey work and determined its usefulness for the modeling and
downstream topographic work (item 4 above) to follow.   Staff recommends approval of this IGA
which authorizes the DISTRICT to reimburse the SCFCD for $4,900, the costs it incurred for the
topographic survey work specified in the IGA.   
 
Fiscal Impact
Fiscal Year: FY 2023

INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT FOR REIMBURSEMENT OF SURVEY WORK 
COMPLETED ON APPROXIMATELY 402 ACRES UPSTREAM FROM THE MILLET 
SWALE DAM 
 
THIS INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT is entered into as of _June 28, 2022 
("Effective Date") by and between the Navajo County Flood Control District, a special taxing 
district organized pursuant to Title 48, Chapter 21 of the Arizona Revised Statutes ("the 
District"), and the Silver Creek Flood Control District, created by an “Order of the Board” of 
the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on May 2, 1966 ("the SCFCD"). 
 
1. Premises. 
 
1.1 The parties are authorized to enter into intergovernmental agreements for joint or 
cooperative action pursuant to A.RS. § 11-952. 
 
1.2 The SCFCD is comprised of Navajo County residents living in the Town of 
Taylor, the Town of Snowflake and unincorporated areas of Navajo 
County.  
 
1.3 The flood protection and safety of residents living in the areas served by the 
SCFCD is of mutual importance to the District and the SCFCD.  
 
1.4 The Board of Directors of the District has determined that it is an appropriate use 
of District funds to reimburse the SCFCD for topographic survey work of 402 acres 
upstream of the Millet Swale dam and has authorized $4,900 for this purpose, 
subject to the terms and conditions of this  Agreement. 
 
2. The District's Obligations. 
 
2.1 The District shall reimburse the SCFCD $4,900 as described in section 1.4 of this 
agreement.  
 
2.1.1 Payment shall be made by the District within 30 days after receipt of the 
reimbursement request.  
 
3. The SCFCD Obligations. 
 
3.1.1 The SCFCD shall provide the reimbursement request(s) in writing, shall 
specify the costs in sufficient detail to enable the District to determine 
whether they are eligible costs, and shall include the certification of the 
SCFCD board of directors that the costs are eligible costs.  
 
4. Indemnification. 
 
To the fullest extent permissible by law, the SCFCD shall indemnify, defend and hold harmless 
the District and its officers, employees and agents from and against any and all claims, actions,

<!-- page 376 -->
2 
 
suits, liabilities, damages, losses and expenses arising out of the design, procurement, 
permitting and survey work associated with this reimbursement request.  
 
5. General. 
 
5.1 This Agreement shall be effective as of the Effective Date and shall remain in effect 
until the SCFCD receives reimbursement of $4,900.. 
 
5.2 Requests for reimbursement, payments and other communications relating to this  
Agreement shall be sent to the intended recipient at the following address.  Either party 
may specify a different address by written notice to the other party: 
 
If to the District: If to the SCFCD: 
Bill Bess Board Member 
Navajo County Flood Control Silver Creek Flood Protection District 
P.O. Box 668 Holbrook, AZ 86025 PO Box 1494, Taylor, AZ 85939 
(928) 524-4387 work  
Bill.bess@navajocountyaz.gov   
 
5.3 In the event of any dispute arising out of this Agreement, the parties agree to make a 
good faith attempt to informally resolve or mediate the dispute before submitting  the 
matter to arbitration pursuant to the provisions of A.R.S. § 12-1518. 
 
5.4 Each party warrants that this Agreement has been duly authorized by its governing body, 
that the officials executing this Agreement on its behalf have been duly authorized to 
do so, and that when so executed this Agreement shall constitute a valid and binding 
obligation of the parties. 
 
5.5 The provisions of A.R.S. § 38-511 are incorporated herein by this reference. 
 
5.6 This Agreement constitutes the entire understanding between the parties with respect 
to its subject matter, superseding any prior or contemporaneous agreements, whether 
oral or written, concerning the same subject matter. This Agreement may be amended 
only by a written document which expressly refers to it and is executed with the same 
formality by both parties. 
 
5.7 This Agreement shall be recorded with the Navajo County Recorder pursuant to A.RS. 
§11-952. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the authorized representatives of the parties have executed 
this Agreement as of the Effective Date.

Board of Supervisors Regular 11. b.        
Meeting Date: 06/28/2022  
Title: Redistricting Plan for the Navajo County Supervisorial Districts and Northland
Pioneer College Precincts
Submitted By: Leah Thomas, Deputy Clerk of the Board of Supervisors
Department: Board of Supervisors
Presented By: Jason Moore
Motion before the Board:
RECONVENE: Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution 24-2022, adopting a plan for
the Redistricting of Navajo County Supervisorial Districts pursuant to A.R.S. §11-212, and
Precincts for the Northland Pioneer Community College District pursuant to A.R.S. §15-1441. 
Background:
A.R.S. § 11-212 provides that the Navajo County Board of Supervisors shall meet each December
following the release of the United States decennial census data for the purpose of defining the
boundaries and limits of each Supervisorial district and further requires that each district shall be
equal or with not more than a ten per cent difference in population.
Through special legislative enactment, that deadline four countywide redistricting was extended to
July 1, 2022. 
 
Recommendation: 
Approval

Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
 
 
 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
A RESOLUTION OF THE BOARD OF SUPERVISORS OF NAVAJO 
COUNTY, ARIZONA, ADOPTING A PLAN FOR THE REDISTRICTING 
OF SUPERVISORIAL DISTRICTS PURSUANT TO A.R.S. § 11-212, AND 
PRECINCTS FOR THE NORTHLAND PIONEER COMMUNITY 
COLLEGE DISTRICT PURSUANT TO A.R.S. § 15-1441 
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 11-212 requires the Board of Supervisors to meet each December 
following the release of the United States decennial census data for the purpose of defining the 
boundaries and limits of each Supervisorial district and further requires that each district shall be 
equal or with not more than a ten per cent difference in population; and 
 
WHEREAS, through special legislative enactment, that deadline four countywide 
redistricting was extended to July 1, 2022 
 
WHEREAS, A.R.S. § 15-1441(A) additionally requires the Board of Supervisors to 
establish precincts for the five-member board of the Northland Pioneer Community College, 
which is also subject to the constitutional principle of “one-person, one vote,” and whereas the 
precincts for the Community College District have traditionally ran along the same lines as the 
Supervisorial precincts; and 
 
WHEREAS, in redistricting the Supervisorial Districts and NPC precincts the Board is 
also subject to certain federal laws, specifically including Sections 2 of the Voting Rights Act, 42 
U.S.C. § 1973 et seq.; and 
 
WHEREAS, after the 2020 U.S. Census, there was a deviation between the population of 
the most populated and least populated Districts of 15.58%, which is in excess of the ten percent 
deviation allowed by A.R.S. § 11-212; and 
 
WHEREAS, curing this imbalance requires to bring Navajo County into compliance with 
A.R.S. § 11-212; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Board established a Redistricting Advisory Committee consisting of 
representatives from each of the five currently existing Supervisorial Districts to advise it and 
make recommendations regarding a new Redistricting Plan for Navajo County, which complies 
with all applicable legal requirements and gives due consideration to the interests of all affected 
parties; and

<!-- page 390 -->
Navajo County - For Official Use Only 
WHEREAS, the Board established criteria to guide the Redistricting Advisory 
Committee in fulfilling its mission; and 
 
WHEREAS, those criteria included as their two highest priorities as (1) compliance with 
the Voting Rights Act and the avoidance of retrogression in the position of racial and language 
minorities with respect to their effective exercise of the electoral franchise, while avoiding 
packing of minority voters in such a way as to create liability under Section 2 of the Voting 
Rights Act; and (2) compliance with A.R.S. § 11-212, and the constitutional principle of “one-
person, one vote” and all applicable case law interpreting that constitutional requirement; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Board also instructed the Redistricting Advisory Committee to attempt 
to achieve, to the greatest extent feasible, the achievement of compact, contiguous districts; the 
following of easily identifiable geographic boundaries; preservation of “communities of 
interest”; the recognition of municipal boundaries and communities of interest; the preservation 
of existing voting precincts; and the due consideration of incumbent-constituency relations; and 
 
WHEREAS, the individual Redistricting Advisory Committee members held twenty-
three (23) community meetings throughout the County, including several meetings on Navajo, 
Hopi and White Mountain Apache tribal areas, and which also included two virtual county-wide 
meetings, and; 
 
WHEREAS, the Redistricting Advisory Committee has given due consideration to the 
comments and proposals of all affected groups; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Redistricting Advisory Committee held three separate public meetings 
where redistricting plans were discussed and considered, and on May 16, 2022, recommended to 
the Board of Supervisors three alternative plans for consideration at a duly noticed public 
meeting; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Board of Supervisors has heard public comments on the proposals and 
met at a final public meeting on June 28, 2022, considered the recommended plans of the 
Redistricting Advisory Committee, the recommendations of the Navajo Nation Human Rights 
Commission (NNHRC); and additional public comments; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Board finds that after consideration of the legal requirements of the 
Voting Rights Act and the constitutional principle of “one-person one vote”, the other 
redistricting priorities enumerated herein, and the input of community members received by the 
Redistricting Advisory Committee and the Board itself, that the Plan attached hereto and 
incorporated by reference is the best redistricting plan that can be adopted in Navajo County at 
this time. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the Board as follows: 
 
1.)  That the redistricting plan attached hereto and incorporated by reference is hereby 
adopted as the redistricting plan of Navajo County for the five Supervisorial Districts 
and Northland Pioneer Community College Precincts, subject to preclearance by the 
United States Department of Justice pursuant to Section 5 of the Voting Rights Act.
